<pre>Let me analyze this protein sequence to determine its biological function.

First, let me look at the sequence characteristics:
- Length: approximately 310 amino acids
- Contains motifs that I need to identify

Let me look for key motifs:

1. &quot;GKASYKMAKFFID&quot; - This looks like it could be part of a nucleotide binding domain. The &quot;GK&quot; motif is often associated with nucleotide binding.

2. &quot;VILPKGSYISLPKVEVIESTHPD&quot; - This contains a &quot;PKGSYIS&quot; motif which is characteristic of phosphoglycerate mutase family.

3. &quot;GGASALM&quot; - This is a classic motif found in phosphoglycerate mutase (PGM) family enzymes.

4. &quot;DINIVLRK&quot; - Found twice, this is part of the active site motif in phosphoglycerate mutase family.

5. &quot;GGEPDVKIEGKAGKGGRNGEVCLGFLKW&quot; - This contains &quot;GGEPD&quot; which is another PGM family motif.

6. The presence of multiple &quot;DINIVL&quot; motifs and &quot;GGASALM&quot; strongly suggests this is a phosphoglycerate mutase (PGM).

Now, the key question is whether this is a cofactor-dependent PGM (dPGM) or cofactor-independent PGM (iPGM):

- Cofactor-dependent PGM (dPGM) uses 2,3-bisphosphoglycerate as a cofactor and catalyzes the reversible interconversion of 2-phosphoglycerate and 3-phosphoglycerate.
- Cofactor-independent PGM (iPGM) does not require a cofactor and also catalyzes the reversible transfer of phosphate between 3-phosphoglycerate and 2-phosphoglycerate.

The cofactor-independent PGM family is characterized by:
- Larger size (~300 amino acids)
